There comes a treacherous virus and a somewhat missed government policy, and we are already ready to put untouchable fundamental rights out of power on time. ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e11837	Madam President. High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Today we are discussing the report of the People's Procuratorate of 2019, a report that is divided. One part deals with all the questions of the so-called auditing control, the control of the public administration. This is that part of the report, which includes those competences which are probably the most well-known competences and tasks of the public prosecutor's office in the public. The second part deals with preventive human rights control, a control task carried out by the People's Procuratorate. It is a task in the constitutional rank, in which the People's Procuratorate, with its commissions, visits places of freedom, visits, and determines whether there is a risk of human rights violations. This is a very important mandate that the People's Procuratorate is carrying out on behalf of Parliament, because – and we attach particular importance to it – the People's Procuratorate is an institution of Parliament, is a control body of this House. We also attach particular importance to this. If, as has already been said by some previous speakers, a little more than 16,000 people turn to the public prosecutor every year because they do not agree with the execution of an administrative process – we say it in general – then it sounds very much. After all, these are between 60 and 80 complaints per working day. First of all, that really sounds huge. However, we would like to stress that this does not mean that Austria is a poorly managed country, because it would have to be compared with the number of administrative acts carried out in our country on a daily basis. This is a number that we cannot count together, because it is probably so huge and because all this – federal administration, state administration and local authorities – is so intertwined. We may well have the impression that Austria is an excellently managed country, some even say an over-managed country. In any case, however, it is not our job to see where everything works, but our task is to look at where people feel unfairly judged. I have already mentioned the number of more than 16 000 complaints a year, of which we were not responsible at 4 500, and with 4,000 we had a procedure without a thorough examination, and with over 8 000 a test procedure was initiated. Of these 8 000 proceedings, just over 5 000 were the federal administration and about 3 000 were the state and local authorities, and as a public prosecutor for the federal states of Tyrol and Vorarlberg, we do not work as national prosecutors – there are national prosecutors of our own, and there we are only examining everything that the Federal Government is going to do. The Federal Administration accounted for about 29 percent of all complaints in the fields of employment, social affairs and health, 22 percent in the field of justice and about 20 percent in the field of internal security. There is also a relatively clear picture of the state and local administration. For example, about 27 per cent of social affairs, 21 per cent of spatial planning and construction, are closely followed by issues of citizenship, the street police and other matters. The second part of the report is this National Prevention Mechanism for the Protection of Human Rights. I've been on it for a while. Here too we were with Our commissions, I think, are quite active. Over 500 such visits took place during the period under review. That's about 10 percent of all facilities. We have about 5,000 such institutions in Austria, where measures to restrict freedom or withdraw freedom are imposed, and each year the public prosecutors and their commissions review about 10 percent of these institutions. We have also carried out accompanying controls on more than 30 police missions. I also allow myself to point out other areas of the public prosecutor's office, as they are often not perceived in the public. We implement the Home-sacrifice Pension Act, within which people who have been abused or abused in children's homes or in youth welfare facilities have the opportunity to receive a small pension. This will also be examined by the Commission. Furthermore, we are responsible for the Convention on the Law of the Disabled to enable people with disabilities to have the same existence, and Austria – the committee president, Ms Diesner-Wais, has pointed out – is the headquarters of the International Ombudsman Institute, a global organisation of parliamentary ombudsmen institutions, in which more than 200 organisations from more than 100 countries now come together to intensify human rights control and cooperation, in particular in the context of human rights. I would like to thank the House for very intensive advice on the two parts of the reports in two days. I think it has also become visible that the People's Procuratorate is an essential control body of the Republic. Of course, we are also trying to expand our competences, because we have no means of control in the area of the distributed bodies of local authorities, as is the case with countries or municipalities. We are a little different from the Court of Auditors. It is mainly about institutions which are still in more or less full possession of these local authorities. It is quite absurd, however, that, if you look at hospitals, we have the test competence in some hospitals because they are not distributed, others do not have them, but may check the patient's lawyer there for example. This seems to be a bit absurd, which is why a request to the legislator is made to carry out an extension of competence. In this sense, I would like to thank you very much for the debate and for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e11920	Madam President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! There is an extensive report that, I believe, gives a very good overview of what citizens in Austria complain about when it comes to their relationship with the administration. In more than 8 000 cases, these complaints are so serious that we contact the administrative authorities and initiate a formal investigation procedure. Fortunately, it can be said that the authorities are very cooperative and that in many cases our intervention leads to the act being more precise Looks at another, more citizen-friendly legal interpretation and that you can solve the problem. However, there are cases in which the authority is unable to resolve the complaint of citizens satisfactorily, because simply the legal situation is such that it is bound and the legislator is required. These are particularly interesting cases for the House from my point of view. I would like to draw your attention to some of these cases. In the field of young families, we have two major problems, which repeatedly arise and lead to frequent complaints at the public prosecutor's office: One problem arises when a young family with a small child lives a partner in Austria and the other works abroad. Then, very often, there are problems with the payment of the childcare allowance. I do not want to bore you with details now, but legally this is not quite easy and, of course, also affects EU law and international law. As a result, however, in very, very many cases, young families have to wait two, three years and, in exceptional cases, much longer for the childcare allowance. This is, of course, an extremely unsatisfying situation, which we consider to be a problem. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Either you do this with appropriate instructions to the ministries or you need a change of law to solve this situation. Another problem with young parents coming to us concerns the mother-child-pass examinations. They are to be proved, and this is, of course, useful, because it is the case that these investigations are to be made to determine whether mother and child are well-informed. In some cases, the studies are carried out and only the evidence is not provided in time. The penalty that the evidence is not provided in time is not entirely insignificant for young families, it is 1 300 euros. For some, the following is particularly disconcerting: They have to provide proof of the local health telephone or now of the Austrian health insurance scheme, but they have carried out the investigations at the institutions of this health insurance institution. The health insurance institution thus knows that the investigations have been carried out, but because the proof is not provided, the penalty of EUR 1 300 occurs. This is true of many people, and I think it is worth dealing with this problem and solving it. A second subject, which I would like to address, is the subject of drugs, which has already been mentioned a few times in the debate. We have cases – especially in nursing homes – in which we are confronted with too many drugs. There are demonstration projects in which pharmacists, doctors, caregivers and home-carriers have joined forces to check how many medications are taken in order to adjust the medication perfectly. This has led to very good results. Legally, this is not so easy if not the agreement of all is made on a good will basis. In order for this to be done everywhere, it also needs a legal basis. In fact, the nursing homes should be obliged to do it everywhere, because it not only saves drug costs, but also increases the well-being of residents, and that is the determining factor. In another case, we find that too few drugs are available. Unfortunately, drug supply bottlenecks occur again and again and again and concern citizens very massively. If the drug that is urgently needed is not present, you have to look for alternatives anyway. However, it also happens when the medicine is not present in the appropriate package size, because more packs mean more prescription fees. This is, in many cases, too little attention. It is also urgently necessary in this area to counter politically. Finally, I would also like to comment on the extension of the test competences: The distributed facilities are becoming more and more. The public prosecutor's great advantage in examining an authority is that any authority is obliged to cooperate with the Public Prosecutor's Office. We have the right, every act, every correspondence, any documentation of the administrative action in the original, and we can then make a judgment. We do not have this right at distributed facilities, we depend there on goodwill cooperation and very often we lack important documents, since they do not need to be transmitted to us and then not transmitted. In this sense, I would like to join the suggestion that many have made here to extend the test competence. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e12005	Madam President. High house! The People's Procuratorate is an institution, an auxiliary body of legislation. We also see that it can only be so that this matter, which is perceived to be unfair, may not have been wanted by the legislator at all. This, in turn, is the interplay that the People's Procuratorate, as your auxiliary body, gives the corresponding suggestion to Parliament, gives to the legislator and says: Perhaps something has happened to you, perhaps the legislator would need to step up his efforts to change unjust solutions for the people of our country. In the field of human rights control, I would like to point out that as a public prosecutor, we serve not only our staff but also the Human Rights Advisory Council, which advises the People's Procuratorate. The Human Rights Advisory Board is a body in which, on the one hand, human rights experts from the field of science and teaching, practitioners from the field of the application or control of human rights in the field of non-governmental organisations, as well as officials, top officials from ministries are represented in order to reconcile the feasible, the necessary and also the legal situation accordingly, because science and teaching on human rights are often already on a very different standard from the case law or legal implementation. The Public Prosecutor's Office serves the work of a total of six commissions, which are persons with a very special qualification from different areas, who can visit these institutions of detention – judicial guards, police inspections and nursing homes – and check the relevant standards and then make recommendations. There are, of course, suggestions from my area, for example from the field of detention or pushyness. Also for you or for the people in front of the screens: It is also a question of human rights, for example, whether there is an emergency bell button or a fire detector in a text box. So even this is predictive of the human right to physical integrity in a cell something essential and important. I think very many citizens do not think that even such a one – under quotation – “smallness” under the protection of human rights can fall. But sometimes, high-level legislators, there are these suggestions, and they fail to have the individual administrative bodies in the context of the separation of powers, say the ministries, for the provision of rooms or for the monitoring of visits or for the implementation of clearances in the Hof et cetera have too little staff or too little budget. That is to say, it is very often so that suggestions made by the public prosecutors must be linked to more posts of service. This is reflected in the reports and then comes to you as a legislator, which you have to decide on the question to which extent you can then release the budgetary possibilities that these measures will also be implemented as soon as possible. I place, the administrative authorities are always interested in implementing the plan, but we get the answer very often: Budgetary resources are currently opposed. ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e15503	We now come to points 6 and 7 of the agenda on which the debates are being held under one. There was no oral reporting. I welcome the President of the Court of Auditors. Mr Johann Singer has now come to speak. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e15536	Mr President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors. Mrs. State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! At a time unusual for Court of Auditors reports, we are dealing with a wide range of reports today. In the context of these agenda items, there are two – the President has already announced it. I would like to take a closer look at the report on supervision of non-profit associations. This review took place in the first half of 2018 with the aim of assessing the performance of the supervisory bodies. A total of 34 recommendations have been made. When we speak of non-profit building associations, we speak of Austria around 180 cooperatives and capital companies that manage about one million apartments and rebuild around 16 500 apartments per year. They are thus an essential factor in the Austrian housing landscape and in the performance of the tasks of the housing public utility law also guarantee for cheaper apartments. Under the Austrian Federal Constitution, the Federal Government is responsible for the implementation of the legislation and the countries in this area. This means that supervision is also in the task of the countries. The Federal Ministry of Digitalization and Economics, the supervision of the respective competent country and the examination of the audit association are specifically observed. If there is a public ownership or control by local authorities, the Court of Auditors shall also have an audit competence. Now back to the specific test report, first to the criticism points and recommendations for countries: It has been found that the countries provide low personnel resources and therefore the examinations of non-profit building associations to a modest extent have not taken place. As a result, a recommendation is also made to the countries to also use independent experts in examinations. The objective allocation of housing was also addressed. It should also be monitored accordingly, and it should be noted that most of the apartments of the non-profit building associations are granted accordingly by the municipalities. Ladies and gentlemen! The report also contains recommendations to us as legislators, and with the novelle of the housing benefit law which we have in Thankfully decided on July 2019, many suggestions have been implemented. I'm allowed to say a few: One essential point, which has been repeatedly called upon by the Court of Auditors, was to strengthen the non-profit commitment. That is to say, the capital raised must not, as it were, flow from speculators, but must be used again in the sense of a generation compensation for non-profit housing. Another point concerns the clarification of the business system, i.e.: Where can the non-profit building association operate and where not? The strengthenings required by the Court of Auditors in the areas of fit and property, compliance and corporate governance have been implemented and will be implemented by 2021. The Federal Ministry for Digitalisation and Economic Location also increased its involvement in the administrative supervision of non-profit associations. On the other hand, although politically planned, the reintroduction of a regulatory requirement test for the recognition of a non-profit building association, as proposed by the Court of Auditors, has not been implemented accordingly. In conclusion, I should like to say: The National Council has largely complied with the Court of Auditors' recommendations. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e15700	Mr President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Today we have very, very many reports from the Court of Auditors on the agenda. At the current item on the agenda there are two things to choose from, and I too am dedicated to this with regard to access to industrial training. In 2017, the Court of Auditors reviewed the aspects of access regulation in relation to the achievement of commercial rights. The aim was to assess the essential conditions for access, to assess the efficiency and costs of administrative processes and to assess the potential for further development, taking into account European conditions. That is where the Court of Auditors comes to some very interesting conclusions. Among other things, it concludes that the trade reform – which took place for the last time in 2017 – has only partially facilitated and continues to miss the trade regulation flexibility and debureaucratisation. The Court also criticises that access to commercial professions is still too vague and bureaucratic. It notes that the structure of the trade regulation has significant weaknesses that would be urgently needed, in the sense of user-friendliness and the use of citizens, because this would, in principle, have a negative impact on the transparency and understanding of the entire regulatory work for citizens. It would encourage interpretation problems and would be appropriate to make it difficult for the competent commercial authorities to implement them, the Court of Auditors notes clearly, and this, although the trade regulation, which has been in place for 150 years, has been ‘averagely almost annually’ and the planned and announced measures for the liberalisation and simplification of access to the profession have often not been implemented in the past. Conclusion: In principle, the trade regulation remains unclear and complex because of the numerous special and special provisions and regulations. It would be urgent to re-codify the rules of commerce – this is one of the main recommendations of the Court of Auditors. In this context, we jump on the train: If the Court of Auditors already makes recommendations, they should also be taken into account and implemented accordingly. I therefore make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] Since it would be consistent to work towards a re-codification of the trade order, I hope that it will be widely accepted. - Thank you. ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e21791	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Dear citizens! In this Court of Auditors report, which was about 3 hours ago and which is now again, we are dealing with the control and supervision of nearly 200 companies, over one million homes. These companies, these non-profit housing companies, work privately but on a public contract. This order is: to create a common well-being living space, so that everyone and every one on the housing market finds the suitable and for him or her also a manageable apartment to rent or buy them. In order to fulfil this task, the GBVs, the non-profit construction associations, are subject to special restrictions. They can only demand rents up to cost coverage, they have to obtain their equity, they have a profit limit, they receive a large amount of about 2 billion euros per year of tax money from the construction of housing, they are also free of costs – that is, several hundred million euros in funding – and they get very often cheap land from the public sector, especially from municipalities. This system has certainly proved itself, but we must constantly improve it: “If you stop wanting to get better, stop being good. ” Many of the Court's proposals in the report on which it is based have already been implemented – that is right, Mr Singer said quite correctly before – but a very central request, a very central proposal has not been implemented, although it has been implemented since 2015, and I am concerned today. A crack point in getting better – we all know that personally – is the control, the feedback, best independent, best to be quite honest. This helps to improve and guarantees that, in the case of institutions such as the GBVs, tax money will be used as determined. Wherever tax money is used, where it is used, we have this independent control. Any cultural club that receives a few thousand euros from a country or a city will eventually be audited by a Court of Auditors or a control office. Although the non-profit housing associations amount to billions of tax money, the Court of Auditors can hardly examine one of these GBVs except for the public sector. All others do not have parliamentary control – neither through a country day nor through the National Council. That means it controls Someone already. But that's exactly where you have to be: They control themselves. You have a revision association. There, the chiefs of the associations to examine the revision association sit on the Board of Directors and on the Supervisory Board. These are the same bosses who, as we can read in this report, are responsible, for example, for the sale of homes to family members or for the placing of orders on themselves. You can read all of this in this report. With the self-control of the GBVs we let the dog look after the sausage, and that may not be our claim. Then there is a second possibility to control, namely by the countries as the supervisory authority. However, this is what the Court of Auditors repeatedly notes, overshadows them, and then turns to the revision body again and again. ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22143	Madam President. Mr President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Dear spectators at home in front of the screens! Having a roof over the head is something undisputed important. Unfortunately, this is not always easy for everyone to deal with. For this very reason Social housing projects as well as funded apartments are very important. In this context, non-profit housing associations are also decisively involved and necessary. From February to July 2018, the Court of Auditors reviewed the supervision of non-profit building associations in Salzburg, Tyrol and the city of Vienna. The Court of Auditors has found that there is a lack of human resources in the supervision. There are shortcomings in compliance reports. These circumstances, as well as other problems and inconsistencies in housing contracts and in the sale of shares of non-profit building associations, allow only one conclusion, namely that there was a friendly economy. There are even some examples of this in the Court of Auditors' report. Ladies and gentlemen! For example, it was approved by the Supervisory Board of a non-profit building association in Tyrol an apartment for sale. The apartment itself had 105 square meters, a basement section as well as two car storage spaces. The price was EUR 438 000. In fact, not this apartment was sold, but an apartment with about 114 square meters plus roof terrace and roof garden with 128 square meters, at a price of 479 608 euros. The buyer of the property was by chance also the wife of the director of the builder. Such actions and actions must be prevented in the future. This cannot be the real meaning of the people's housing system, and I therefore hope that the Court of Auditors' recommendations will be implemented as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22258	The President of the Court of Auditors has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Kraker.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22280	Madam President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! There are two reports from the Court of Auditors on key issues of the economic sector. These are access to professional professional training and supervision of non-profit building associations. This concerns, on the one hand, the subject of the trade regulations, the heart of the economic resort, and, on the other hand, the housing law on public utility. The report on access to industrial training has highlighted aspects of access regulations in connection with the acquisition of commercial rights in Austria. It represents in particular, taking into account the European Commission's requirements, what progress has been made in terms of regulated professions and what is still open. There was the trade reform 2017. Since this reform, there have been around 100 regulated trades. Instead of the single commercial license, which was intended for all free trade, there is a commercial license, so to speak, a list of trade. The reform has therefore implemented only a part of the originally planned facilitation for traders. What the Court of Auditors has found is what has been said more often in the debate – you know it even better than I do –: The trade regulations are being modernised almost annually, and this creates a very complex regulatory framework. We also recommend a re-codification of the trade order from the point of view of user-friendliness. We believe that the lack of ease of use and complexity also contributed to the fact that the online applications and the possibilities of the Gisa web form were used very little, namely to 11 percent, and that very often the applications for the founding service of the Chamber of Commerce and the support of the Chamber of Commerce were claimed. In the case of enforcement, it is the case that the trade regulation is carried out in a medium-sized federal administration, which can be associated with the fact that the enforcement of commercial law could be different and thus be documented differently. With regard to the uniformity of professional access, it is important to us that there is a uniform commercial law enforcement throughout the country and that the overview in the Ministry on this uniform commercial law implementation is also available and that it is used by the regulatory competence – controlling competence, on the one hand, that the implementation experiences from the countries involve a possible legal development, and on the other hand, with regard to the taking of deregulation initiatives. The report was published on the subject of the dual vocational training system. I would really like to say that the Court of Auditors did not question this in any way. On the contrary: We have highlighted very positively that the Master's exams are now included in the National Qualifications Framework. We also welcome the fact that the Ministry is in favour of a quality management recommended by the Court of Auditors for this master and qualification examination. That the single and central commercial register has replaced 14 decentralised registers we have also highlighted positively, but of course there is also – it has been said more often – development potential with regard to data maintenance and data quality and also with regard to the use of data from the commercial register. The second report concerns the exercise of supervision over non-profit building associations, on non-profit-making housing companies. This is the test report that concerns, on the one hand, the economic sector, which is responsible for the legislation – the housing law and the regulations – and, on the other hand, the countries responsible for the implementation of the housing sector. On the one hand, they grant housing promotion, and on the other hand they also take care of the housing owners. Supervision is divided into two parts: on the one hand, supervision of management and conduct review, and on the other hand, there are legally liable transactions. In the course of the examination, we have found that countries have very few resources to monitor construction associations. The resources are very low. It varies between 0.4 and 1.5 full-time jobs in the countries of Tyrol, Salzburg and Vienna, which are used for this task. How is that possible? – It is therefore possible to rely on the expertise of the revision association, and the revision association is at the same time the review body of the members, but also their representation of interests. In this sense, the motion for a resolution - whose full reading you have requested, Madam President - has already been addressed, as the control architecture in this area looks like, and it is precisely so that the Court of Auditors is only responsible for checking 25 of the 185 non-profit housing companies. This is due to the fact that we need a public share, a majority, in order to enable the Court of Auditors to be audited. Of course, non-profit-making housing companies work very strongly with public funds et cetera, so we believe that an audit of the Court of Auditors would be consistent. As far as the Ministry is concerned, we have noted that some of the terms in the housing law and in the implementing provisions are in need of interpretation and therefore make problems in execution time and again. These are the connex supplementary transactions, the concept of members of the construction industry and the indirect and direct acquisition of shares. Similarly, the provisions on ancillary transactions would have to be revised – this also leads to excessively long procedures. In addition, the Court of Auditors calls for the mandatory application of a corporate governance code for the non-profit housing sector to ensure responsible corporate governance. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e23155	We arrive at the 13th. Agenda item. There was also no oral reporting. Our colleague Andreas Kühberger is now speaking. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e23185	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mr President-in-Office of the Council! Ladies and gentlemen! Estimated Austrians! I should now like to comment on a report from the Court of Auditors which, in my opinion, deals with a very important and special issue. It's about reading at our schools in Austria. To this end, the Court of Auditors has examined the Federal Ministry of Education, Science and Research, as well as the State School Councils in Lower Austria and Salzburg. They have looked closely at the measures taken by the two countries in the field of reading. The period from 2014/15 to 2017/18 was reviewed. The results of the studies show that, unfortunately, mistakes have been made over and over again in promoting reading skills in the past. Since 2003, Austria has been cutting off more below average in the OECD Pisa study, and only in 2018 has it been again in the OECD average for the first time. Moreover, after the end of secondary school I, i.e. the primary school, 17 percent of children can handle only the simplest reading tasks, and this, although 30 percent of the teaching hours – i.e. 30 percent of the resources – have been spent on subjects of German, reading and writing. The Court of Auditors also notes the reasons for this and says that parental education can have a strong impact. Children with migration backgrounds, who do not have German as their mother tongue, are also classified as a risk group. Ladies and gentlemen, these different start-up conditions were not actually taken into account. The report particularly criticises that the curriculum would have been too general and that there were no uniform performance assessments. The consequences are that 970 000 Austrians have very little reading skills, 240 000 even have an extremely low level. Of course, people often have disadvantages in their everyday lives. I think it is our common goal to increase the reading skills of Austrian students in a sustainable way. In order to achieve these objectives, the Court recommends that action be taken on the following priorities: The curriculum and the principle of learning should be revised in practice, a uniform quality assurance and quality management should be developed, as well as increased use of control and monitoring. Education directives in Lower Austria and Salzburg are recommended to use a person on the subject of reading. The overall aim of the Court of Auditors is therefore to join forces in the national strategy to promote reading skills. Although the Court of Auditors also mentions positively that a large number of stakeholders are involved in reading, I do not think that this fact is sufficiently taken into account in the proposed measures. Overall, however, the report looks very positive, he also sees it positive that the Ministry under Minister Fäsmann has already implemented many of its recommendations. We have heard a good news in the last few days about German classes: 80 percent of students can attend regular lessons after a year, 32 percent of them actually no longer need additional care. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] These are examples that show that different measures are necessary, because every student has different requirements, and one does not go as before when all students were thrown into a pot and one simply hoped or assumed that they met somewhere at a language level. Above all, children with migration backgrounds, who have to pick up step by step in the first phase, were very disadvantaged. As the example shows, these German classes are really a success story. I hope that the critics will now also be muted. I think we are very good on the road as far as reading skills and also education in Austria are concerned. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e24063	Mr President. High house! We have compared the situation in two countries: Lower Austria and Salzburg. Reading is more than ever a cultural technique that needs to be maintained, especially in the age of digitization, and reading skills is a central ability that is important for the personal and professional development of each individual. The audit focus of the Court of Auditors is on the quality of public service, and from this point of view we have also looked at the promotion of reading. We have seen whether the measures are in place at schools, especially in the primary and secondary schools, i.e. until the 8th century. School level – to provide students with sufficient reading skills, which is the focus of the report. The Pisa tests in recent years show that reading skills tend to decline and that, since 2000, under-average reading results have been achieved among 15 to 16-year-old students. The Pisa-Test 2018 also brought no trend change – it was evident that the OECD-section has also dropped overall. For adults, for 16- to 65-year-olds, we see that 17 percent have low reading skills. That is why we have to make sure that we do the right thing is done enough. In the Court's view, structured and coordinated measures – a whole set of measures – are needed to increase the quality of reading lessons. This includes, first of all, the fact that this provision of principle, which has already been mentioned today, can also be implemented and implemented in practice. The Austrian Framework Reading Plan, which was developed with high personnel input, was stopped in 2016. This lacks a national strategy based on operational objectives to promote reading skills. The education standard review showed that 13 percent of pupils in primary schools did not even partially reach the standards; in secondary education, it was 17 percent – i.e. the results of the 8th. School level has worsened – and the NMS is 24 percent. Studies and tests show that there are read risk groups. These are boys who read worse than girls, students with migration backgrounds, children of parents with low levels of education. There are targeted, right and sustainable measures to be taken. It is about strengthening the group of powerful readers and reducing the proportion of children with lower performance. Of course, this is also the question of the Covid crisis: How did the group of those who could hardly be reached by the school develop during this time? – I think we are already in the process of introducing appropriate measures, and in general we are concerned about whether we are doing enough for the younger generation as a whole. The importance of the school libraries has also been addressed. These can, of course, make a significant contribution to the promotion of reading. There should also be a further development towards an active role, such as involvement in the field of day care. In developing education standards and combining them with informal competence measurement, it is also important to us – in the Court's view – that evidence-based support for schools in quality development is still possible. It is about comparability with the training standards already carried out, because there are data stocks in this area which are already built up and which should continue to be used. It's not supposed to be lost. I would also like to address the use of resources. For school success, it is important that sufficient teaching time is available. That's understood, I think, by itself. We found that there was a reduction in weekly hours in 2003, but the effects were not analysed. We have also found that resource use – especially in public schools – is below the OECD average. It is therefore appropriate to evaluate whether these hourly reductions may have an impact on the basic competences of pupils. – Thank you for the attention.

Thus, the thick study consultant should no longer be printed, but be available online. It is also planned to combine the two study selection advisory websites in the future, thus saving administrative resources that can then be used in other areas. Our goal must also be to further improve the student choice advice. That is why it is clear to me that it is best to do what you like, and that is why we need to bring young people there exactly. That is why it is also important and right that we continue to invest in vocational and study orientation and continuously improve it.

The one recommendation which concerns the documentation of the conditions of the proposed candidates for the work as university councils proposed by the Ministry is now to be taken up, according to the statements of the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs, which I consider to be pleasing. Furthermore, the proper application of the travel fee requirement is open. That's where we keep our recommendation. As far as the implementation rate is concerned, this is certainly to be seen quantitatively, but the Court of Auditors has long been trying to make a qualitative evaluation. This is what we have done in the activity report, in which we have stated in detail which recommendations are not implemented in a sustainable way and are therefore open as key recommendations. We will continue to do so. I would like to draw attention to the report on secondary employment of university professors and university professors, which has already been discussed in detail. These are jobs outside the service. These are, of course, permissible, but we have identified gaps in the reports and we have identified the need for action, in so far as measures to raise the reporting morale still need to be imposed, as a regular reminder of the reporting obligations, and as for example in the operating agreement the concept of the essential service interests of universities must be defined in order to also have a uniform handling of the possible prohibition of secondary employment. It is precisely a good report of secondary employment in order to preserve the service interests of universities. We have found that at the University of Vienna about half as many secondary jobs have been reported compared to the University of Innsbruck, although the University of Vienna employs twice as many university professors. However, we can also say that we have seen it as an immediate success that the reports at the University of Vienna have already increased significantly in the course of our on-the-spot review. Therefore, our examination could raise awareness of this obligation to deliver messages. In the course of a follow-up review, we will actually determine whether the trend is continuing. I would also like to draw attention to the Zurich model. We presented this model. This is a transparent system in the form of a register of commitments, according to which employment in general is regarded as secondary employment accessible on the Internet. The creation of similar transparency rules could also be remembered in Austria. There is no locality in working at universities, so monitoring is difficult, but of course it could have an influence on teaching. There were problems at the University of Innsbruck over and over again, the proven course evaluations that took place there. At the University of Vienna, which we also criticized, these questions – whether a course started on time, whether the holding was carried out regularly – were even excluded from the course evaluation. We don't think it should be. That is, we believe that there should always be processes, in which the complete holding of courses is thoroughly examined by the universities. The report on choice of studies deals with the transition management between school and universities, the tertiary sector. That's where the interaction has to work. There are the projects Maturantinnenberatung of the Austrian University School, the programme 18plus for the selection of students, the student and educational advice. There was only one voluntary participation, with a maximum of 64 percent of the schools participating. We also believe that the programme coverage is still expandable. I thank you for your support for the Court of Auditors in the debate and for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e25542	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seer in front of the screens! The two proposals we are now discussing on this item on the agenda may not have much to do at first sight, but at the second, more precise, as I mean. On the one hand, it is a request for the Federal Government to conduct a survey on the gender impact of the Corona crisis, on the other hand, we want to ensure that the National Action Plan on Women's Health is financed and forced. Both applications indicate that women are affected differently from men, both from the corona crisis and when it comes to maintaining their health or treating their diseases. That is why, in both thematic areas, a differentiated view of things needs to be taken. Ladies and gentlemen, let me first say a few sentences on the effects of the Corona crisis. There are already initial studies on the gender impact of the crisis – one of the OECD, which Mrs Ecker has already said – and they warn against the fact that the crisis could at least reinforce the existing inequalities between man and woman in the short term. Many women have also confirmed this impression to me in various personal discussions. It is therefore important that we get well-processed Austrian data to derive the appropriate measures from them. There are a lot of stressed words at present that crises also open up opportunities, but we want to take advantage of them and take them for the benefit of women. I would like to devote the rest of my speaking time to women's health. Women are different from men, and women live longer, but have less healthy years. For example, because of the hormone estrogen, women have a stronger immune system because it inhibits the proliferation of viruses. This also proves the current data on the infections with the coronavirus. However, women are also ill due to their physiology, so they also need a different medical treatment. It is not enough to assume that the woman – under quotation marks – is “the smaller man”. Diseases often occur in women other than in men, but in medicine, the man is still standard; this starts with the investigation of diseases. Austria has already recognised the need for action a few years ago, and by means of the Women's Health Action Plan, it has created a unique approach across Europe that focuses on women's health. The Experts developed 17 impact targets and 40 actions of the Action Plan take into account all stages of life of women. We know that health is influenced by many factors and differences between men and women are essential in the detection and treatment of diseases. The Women's Health Action Plan addresses these differences and shows opportunities for a life-related understanding of health and disease as well as the promotion of a healthy life. It is important – and that is my personal concern – to strengthen women's health skills so that they can make self-determined and informed decisions. This includes, for example, a positive self-image of young women and girls. This is an area in which a lot of things still need to be done, which is recognized by considering, for example, what pressure is already exerted on young girls today because of their appearance, especially through social media. We also call on women over 60 in particular to be better supported in order to combat this initially mentioned difference that women have less healthy years than men. Our Lady Minister Raab is very concerned about the mental health of women. Especially during the Corona crisis, the psychological strains were and have increased significantly, and this has made sure that consulting shotlines and services have been increased. But this alone will certainly not be enough, we need a clear and, above all, sustainable increase in psychological care – not only for women. As part of the Committee on Budgets, we also had the opportunity to discuss this with Mr Anschober, and he assured us that he is aware that there is a great need for action in this area of psychological care. As the last point, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to address gender medicine. We in Tyrol have a chair for gender medicine at the university as well as a women's health clinic at the University Clinic in Innsbruck. There, women are cared for internally under gender-medical conditions. I would like it to be great and it would be so very important if in the near future it could be possible to give gender medicine in medical studies and health care the importance it would need. It is important to me to conclude that gender-based medicine would not only benefit women. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-17-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00036_d7e27109	Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. First of all, I would like to thank you very much for the very constructive discussions in the equal treatment committee. I appreciate the cooperation with you, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] I would like to address two issues which have also been discussed on a number of occasions in the Committee on Equal Opportunities - rightly discussed - and I would like to do something about these issues, which will be dealt with in the Corona crisis, but of course also in a sustainable way for the future. The first issue is the protection of women, the second is women's health. Violence against women – and I believe it is really important that we always get this into our consciousness – is a universal, social problem that affects all women, no matter what age, origin, religion or skin color; and violence against women is not a marginal phenomenon. We all know – an EU-wide survey shows that every fifth woman is affected by physical and/or sexual violence. Of course, it is also a matter of starting from the words that we are already starting with the psychological injuries and verbal injuries. This is not only my concern in women's policy, but at the end of the day it is about: How do we want to live together in a society? How do we want to talk and deal with each other in a society? But especially during the Covid Pandemic and the associated restrictions, the protection of violence – even in the opinion of experts – was a major issue from the beginning. As a result of the social restrictions and restrictions in the living room, of course, many women were in danger, and experts have warned women that they may no longer believe they could go to a women's home, there are no more advisory facilities, there may be no more help they might be able to visit, because the initial restrictions would have closed such facilities. We wanted to inform women from the beginning that they receive help and support at every day and night. That is why we launched a comprehensive information campaign at an early stage. In a cooperation with the trade association, we have rolled out the information in the supermarkets and have informed women that the women's homes in which they receive help and support are open to them that the 24-hour women's help line is available for them, that it will be increased in human and financial terms and will also be available in different languages. We see from our figures that this information offensive has been very well accepted and that, we hope, many women have received preventive support in the crisis. The figures also show: Yes, there has been a slight increase in the figures – and even now after the end of the baseline restrictions – in terms of bans on entry and directions. In April and May, for the first time, we have more than 1,000 such bans. This is not a significant increase, but a slight trend that we must observe very closely. Our information has been well accepted. I'm convinced we've reached a lot of women. We have recorded a 43-percent increase in the women's help line, and our online chat has also been well accepted, because there are many women who cannot call because they are not alone at home, and there is often a better means of writing. One thing I would like to say, though, is that I am talking about the basic limitations and the corona crisis: Yes, the corona crisis has been a huge multiple burden for many women and for many families – I would like to deal with it later on – but very many families have mastered this situation well for themselves and for their children as well. With regard to violence, I would like to further point out that there has been a regular increase in the women's budget for the first time since 2010. It is also my concern that we continue to strengthen the protection of violence and the protection of victims. I am convinced that – and this has confirmed to me, for example, the UN Special Commissioner for Violence Protection – in Austria we have a good network of women's and girls' advisory facilities, which will advise women and girls in all situations of life. It was also my concern to strengthen this system. Accordingly, I have given all the advisory facilities for women and girls a 12 percent increase, which is already distributed. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] Furthermore, just a few days ago, I made a so-called funding call in the volume of 1.25 million euros, thereby promoting projects to protect women and girls. This is not just an exit from the Corona crisis, where we have seen how important it is to strengthen preventive violence. For me, the most important thing is that every woman can live her life by herself. The most important basis for a self-determined life is a non-violent life. Accordingly, we will also strengthen the protection of violence with a new call for funding of 1.25 million euros. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] In addition, we will also make money available from the integration budget for the interface between women and integration and for the fight against culturally-related violence on women and girls. I am also concerned with the children: children's ewes, forced ewes, but also women's genital mutilation. We will also provide an additional 2 million euros for this interface and the prevention against cultural violence. Of course, my cooperation with all the parties, but also with the competent national councils and with all the other ministries in this area is also very important to me. Violence and the prevention of violence are a social issue, it is in itself a cross-cutting matter, not just a matter of the Minister of Women, but a matter of all people in Austria, all ministries, all federal states, all levels. I am really happy and proud that as a woman's minister, I can rely on a government programme in which equality between men and women is reflected in all thematic areas, for this is a living women's policy. In all thematic areas, the women's perspective needs to be considered and discussed centrally. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] A second issue that has been discussed in detail in the Committee – which I am very happy about, because I believe it is really a topic of the future – is women's health, which is also very dear to me personally. Women's health is not only about the prevention and treatment of diseases that only women can affect, such as cervical cancer, or also the care in the event of pregnancy and maternity, but also about women's health is also central to the self-determination of women in all health matters and, of course, gender-specific aspects of prevention and care. The problem of disease, the symptoms of disease, the practice of prescription, the effect and tolerability of medications, all of which often depend on biological, social and cultural factors, which, of course, differ between men and women. Not least, the different risk and health behaviour also plays a role. We also see that the numbers of many diseases, which are thought to have always been male diseases, such as cardiovascular diseases or lung cancer, have been understood in women on the rise. That is why I am grateful that, with Minister of Health, Rudolf, I should be able to continue to strengthen and bring these issues into the future. Let me conclude by stressing that the Corona crisis, as I have already said, was a huge challenge for all people in our country and especially for many women in the compatibility of home schooling, home office and household. I think there is nothing to thank for, and I would like to take every opportunity to thank women as women's minister for what they have done in the crisis. I am also quite clear that we now have to reflect consistently on women's perspectives in all areas of life, because women represent half of the workforce and account for more than half of the population. Accordingly, as the Federal Government, we have of course also created all the measures in the comprehensive discharge package with a women's perspective, namely the reduction of small and medium-sized incomes by reducing the first wage tax level from 25 to 20 percent for 2020, already retroactive from September. For those who do not pay a wage tax due to their lower incomes, there will also be a relief by a negative tax amounting to EUR 100. And for the special support of families there is a child bonus of 360 euros per child. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] If you calculate all of this by using practical examples, you will see very well how much substance is behind it, and then you will see that a family with two children experience an average of 1 000 euros in additional relief through these measures. Of course, these measures benefit in particular women, mothers and single parents. In addition to the crisis aid such as the corona short work, the family hard work fund and the special care period, we are now dealing with the discharge. It is my job as a Minister of Women, which I take very seriously, that we should consider the perspective of women in all these discharge and further package of measures, which we will adopt as a government. I assure you that my passion for women's policy in Austria is valid, and I am very much looking forward to further cooperation with you, ladies and gentlemen.

The study authors also suspect a high number of dark digits in the rise of depressive diseases. But the sufferers are not only the mentally ill, but also their relatives. It is women who are disproportionately affected when the partner falls into an emotional crisis. The most common motives of action are patriarchal possession, jealousy, control, power – all the motives, which are especially in times of great uncertainty, associated with fears of existence, tend to increase rather than decrease. There are also initial signs that confirm this presumption. In Compared to the previous year, there are over 50 percent more bans; Violence prevention facilities throughout Austria report that they have long reached the limits of their capacity. Therefore, the request concerning the continuation of the National Action Plan on the Protection of Women from Violence is not only welcome, but also urgently necessary. In addition to women, children are also very affected. As the Grevio report clearly states, the number of children in women's homes is equal to or often higher than that of women. However, women's homes lack staff, especially when it comes to childcare. It is especially a painful point that there are not enough places for male youths over 14 years old. In these cases the boys sometimes have to continue to live with their violent fathers, where they are subjected to violence every day. Moreover, there is still no sufficient recognition of the psychological damage that children are done through the coexistence of domestic violence. Protection measures should be amended. The conditions for civil action in the event of psychological violence should be adapted in order to facilitate the exercise of their protection function by the authorities. [[Congratulations with representatives of the SPÖ.]] The fact that bans on the protection of children against violence are only relatively rarely adopted gives rise to the suspicion that there is also an increased need for action. Protection of women and children must go hand in hand, only in this way can a family be helped holisticly and above all quickly and unbureaucratically. As the shadow report further points out, intervention agencies and anti-violence centres receive funding only for the support of children directly affected by violence, but not for children who simply have to experience violence. In 2015, for example, the Vienna intervention agency supported 474 children affected by violence in the family, but 5,733 children, who experience violence daily, could not be cared for due to lack of resources. These children and young people often have to deal with traumatic experiences without support. Thus, applications concerning high-risk conferences and the continuation of the National Action Plan on the Protection of Women from Violence are more than contemporary. However, there must be urgent additions, especially those that improve the protection of children. Only in this way can we help not only today, but also ensure that the potential victims of today will not be the perpetrators of tomorrow through an untreated trauma. Please create a framework that makes it easier for affected women to take their courageous step into a non-violent future for themselves and their children, a step that is truly no easier, a step in which we all cannot leave here alone – in a world characterized by anger, pain and fear, in a world in which a mother is often not even possible, whether for reasons of psychological or physical violence to protect her own child! The women and children concerned have a damn right to let us do everything possible here in Parliament in order to ensure this protection! Thank you.

Gender entry - finally implement VfGH knowledge! ==References====External links== Meeting of the National Council on the report of the Committee on Equal Treatment on Amendment No 593/A(E) by Mr Yannick Shetty, colleagues on Put a strong sign against hate crimes and discrimination against LGTIQ persons (213d.B.)– TOP 22 Since the recognition of the Federal Constitutional Court in July 2018 (G77/2017 to § 2 para. 2 Z 3 PStG 2013) in Austria, as well as in 19 other countries in the world, it is theoretically possible to choose a third category of gender in the central register ZPR in addition to the gender categories "male" and "female". Although constitutionally recognised, the third gender category remains, in fact, an utopia. To blame for this is a precept of the former. Interior Ministers Herbert Kickl to the Offices in December 2018. This shows, on the one hand, that the limited scope of the ministry software as a third category of gender is merely the term "divers" - an unnecessaryly narrow version of the term of the third sex, to which the recognition of the VfGH gives much more scope. On the other hand, the third category is to be awarded exclusively "on the basis of a relevant medical opinion" through specific "interdisciplinary and multi-professional medical expert groups" and "interdisciplinary and multi-professional medical groups". Boards. According to an article in the daily newspaper The standard of 23.12.20191, the same boards are still not set up a year later, with which, in fact, one and a half years after the constitutional recognition, the person concerned still does not have the possibility to have the third category actually registered. Not only are such mandatory medical advice from LGTIQ organizations, such as the HOSI Salzburg, classified as "pathological" and "potentially traumatic" and therefore strictly rejected, but they are obviously used by their mandatory character while at the same time non-constitution of the boards necessary for this purpose to simply avoid the recognition of the Constitutional Court. This weakness is unbearable for those concerned, especially since the situation has long been settled by constitutional law and can therefore only be regarded as a shock. In addition, many countries, such as Norway, Portugal, Malta, Belgium, Denmark and Ireland, do not have any dissuasive compulsory medical examinations, but rely on the self-esteem of the persons concerned.2 In addition, the Oberösterreichische Landesverwaltungsgericht (LVwG-750727/5/MZ) decided in respect of this decree that such a "not suitable to justify rights and obligations for legal subjects, and that the Administrative Court is not subject to a binding decision". 1 https://www.derstandard.at/story/2000112544005/suspension-kickls-as-minister-blocked-entry-of-the-third-sex; 2 https://www.salzburg24.at/news/salzburg/third-sex-hosi-critic-am-compulsory physician test-62674825; The honourable Members therefore tabled the following motion for a resolution: The National Council wants to decide: "The Federal Government, in particular the Federal Minister for the Interior, is invited to take all necessary steps to the recognition of the Constitutional Court of July 2018 (G77/2017 to § 2 para. 2 Z 3 PStG 2013) complete and immediate implementation. This includes the abolition of the decree of the former Minister of the Interior Herbert Kickl, which prevents the implementation of the highest court judgment and continues discrimination between the sexes."
